# PULITIKLAN — Visayas coverage and evidence audit

Reviewed 13 September 2026. Each of the **16 Visayas provinces and seven independent cities** has a separate evidence review below. The working inventory grew from **26 to 134 family networks, 79 to 442 family profiles and 55 to 376 documented relationship pairs**. Including independent leadership entries, there are 629 distinct Visayas profiles and 399 sourced portraits. This is a completed locality-by-locality evidence pass, not a claim that every municipal or barangay family has been identified.

## Scope and limits

Every mapped province has a governor and vice-governor roster; every mapped independent city has a mayor and vice-mayor roster. These 46 leadership entries are separate from family membership. They are not complete legislative, municipal, barangay or appointed-office rosters. A person may appear in a family tree and the office roster with the same identifier. City and province records can share a family network without implying jurisdiction over an independent city.

The family networks are a documented starting set, not a census of Visayas political families. Historical generations, municipal dynasties, in-laws, additional siblings and other less documented links require further research. Cebu City’s Osmeña pair, for example, does not purport to show the complete historical Osmeña lineage. A lighter dot can reflect less research. It does not establish fewer actual relatives or less influence.

A line records a published family relationship. It does not allege illegal conduct, biological proof, ownership of an office or control over a territory. The darker-dot bands are unchanged: 1–3, 4–9, 10–19 and 20+ unique documented pairs. Both current and historical links contribute. The green badge means a sourced current office; former living officials have no status badge, and confirmed deceased profiles have a separate label and grayscale image.

## Office evidence

The [DBM 2026 Government Directory](https://www.dbm.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/AboutDBM/2026-Government-Directory.pdf#page=192) identifies its LGU data as **30 September 2025**. The edition year is not treated as a fresh September 2026 verification date for every entry. Its 2025–2028 leadership entries were checked against the cited election outcomes and later available government/news records. Sources and their dates remain linked. The roster is not connected to a live election or appointment feed.

- All 16 governor/vice-governor pairs appear on PDF page 195. Iloilo City is on page 201; Bacolod, Cebu City and Lapu-Lapu are on page 202; Mandaue, Ormoc and Tacloban are on page 203.
- [Cebu’s official governor page](https://www.cebu.gov.ph/governors-office/) names Pamela Baricuatro. She is shown as governor without inventing a family network for her.
- [Bacolod’s July 2026 city report](https://bacolodcity.gov.ph/mayor-gasataya-delivers-first-soca-unveils-heartmore-agenda-for-a-stronger-bacolod/) names Greg Gasataya, Claudio Jesus Puentevella and Albee Benitez. The city’s Puentevella spelling replaces the DBM directory’s Puentebella variant.

## Conflicting and changed records resolved

- **Siquijor:** Jake Vincent Villa is governor; his father Zaldy Villa is representative. A regional summary reversed their offices. [Manila Bulletin · Villa father and son reelected](https://mb.com.ph/2025/05/17/villa-reelected-for-second-term-in-siquijor)
- **Northern Samar:** Harris and Edwin Ongchuan are recorded as cousins. The brothers claim in a separate regional report is excluded. [Rappler · Ongchuan cousins and Evardone father–son](https://www.rappler.com/philippines/elections/2025-races-monitor-samar/)
- **Eastern Samar:** Ralph Vincent Evardone is the current governor, with former governor Ben Evardone as his father. The release does not repeat a summary’s inaccurate reelection description. [Rappler · Ongchuan cousins and Evardone father–son](https://www.rappler.com/philippines/elections/2025-races-monitor-samar/); [DBM · Philippine Government Directory 2026](https://www.dbm.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/AboutDBM/2026-Government-Directory.pdf)
- **Christina Frasco:** the profile uses the March 2026 appointment as Presidential Adviser for Sustainable and Resilient Communities, rather than the earlier tourism-secretary title. [PCO · Christina Frasco new appointment](https://pco.gov.ph/news_releases/president-marcos-appoints-secretary-frasco-as-presidential-adviser-for-sustainable-and-resilient-communities/)
- **Romualdez:** Martin’s role is Leyte first-district representative, without carrying forward an outdated Speaker title. Yedda and Andrew are included as current Tingog party-list representatives and labeled national office. [MindaNews · 20th Congress Tingog representatives and family](https://mindanews.com/special-reports/2026/03/in-numbers-dynasties-and-contractors-capture-party-list-seats-in-the-20th-congress/)
- **Negros Oriental:** governor Manuel “Chaco” Sagarbarria and Dumaguete mayor Manuel “Chikiting” Sagarbarria are separate father-and-son profiles; their offices and photographs are not interchanged. [PCIJ · Negros families](https://pcij.org/2025/05/18/in-region-of-sugar-barons-political-families-rule/)
- **Capiz:** an additional Castro relationship with conflicting degree/name descriptions is not inferred. Only the sourced spouses and daughter links are included.

## Geographic coverage

Boundaries come from the [GeoRiskPH PSA 2020 province layer](https://ulap-nga.georisk.gov.ph/arcgis/rest/services/PSA/Provincial_2020/MapServer/0) and [city layer](https://ulap-nga.georisk.gov.ph/arcgis/rest/services/PSA/Municipal_2020/MapServer/0). Regional grouping reflects the re-established Negros Island Region, including Negros Occidental, Negros Oriental and Siquijor; Bacolod has a separate city record. See [PSA NIR classification](https://psa.gov.ph/classification/psgc/provinces/1800000000). Historical shapes are indicative, not a legal survey.

## Locality-by-locality audit

Each review compares the initial family inventory with election reports, government rosters, biographies and published kinship accounts. Office status and relationship evidence are checked separately. An election candidacy alone does not establish a current office. Search leads below are not verified family memberships and do not create map connections. Historical families without a confirmed current officeholder are also kept separate from the visible-map rule.

### Aklan

**Review status:** Evidence pass completed; the following leads remain unresolved.

- The initial Aklan record omitted the Marquez succession and the Calizo–Quimpo network.
- The Miraflores tree omitted current Ibajay mayor Jose Miguel, former mayor Maria Lourdes and the cousin relationship to former representative Teodorico Haresco.
- A 2025 nomination is not a seat: Maria Lourdes is not assigned a current House badge.

**Sources reviewed for this locality**

- [Aklan Provincial Government · local officials](https://aklan.gov.ph/local-government/) — Current directory accessed 13 September 2026.
- [Tara Yap, Manila Bulletin · Miraflores and Marquez families retain power](https://mb.com.ph/2025/05/15/miraflores-marquez-families-retain-power-in-aklan) — 15 May 2025.
- [Tara Yap, Manila Bulletin · Ibajay mayor identified as governor’s brother](https://mb.com.ph/2025/08/26/murdered-vice-mayor-in-aklan-laid-to-rest) — 26 August 2025.
- [COMELEC · Maria Lourdes Miraflores nomination and spouse declaration](https://www.comelec.gov.ph/php-tpls-attachments/2025NLE/COC_2025NLE/COC_Partylist162/156.%20USWAG%20ILONGGO.pdf) — 2025 election nomination.
- [DBM · historical municipal directory](https://www.dbm.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/DBM%20Publications/Directory2014/Local%20Govt/Municipal.pdf) — 2014.
- [OpinYon · Aklan budget and Calizo–Quimpo family connection](https://opinyon.net/national/aklan-operates-on-a-p2-989-b-budget-this-year) — 2025.

**Open checks — no unverified edges added**

- The degree of relationship between board members Mark and Reynaldo Quimpo remains unverified.
- Prescilla Gale Calizo’s relationship to Dexter must be sourced rather than inferred from surname.
- Jose Haresco resigned from the provincial board in July 2025; verify his family degree before adding a historical node.
- Navarosa, Gubatina, Refol, Ramos, Iquiña and Legaspi municipal/historical leads require individual kinship evidence. Official rosters establish offices, not kinship.

- A 2010 PIA report calls an unnamed San Policarpo councilor the governor’s first cousin; Rappler 2022 lists Boni Nicart and others as “Nicart clan” members without degrees.
- The PCL president’s surname appears as Pomida, Pormida and Pormeda; he is treated as Oras Councilor Irvin Pormida, the only councilor of that name.
- Council names are Comelec ballot names, some of them nicknames; full legal names were not available for councilors.

### Samar

**Review status:** Evidence pass completed; the following leads remain unresolved.

- The initial Tan-only coverage omitted the Catbalogan Uy father–son network.
- 15 September 2026: the Tan network now records Ricardo Tan (Milagrosa’s husband), his brothers Ruben (1995–1998 board substitute) and Arnold (vice governor, so the siblings’ uncle), eldest daughter Angelie (Calbayog mayoral candidate 2013 and 2016) and Stephany Uy-Tan’s estranged marriage to Jimboy Tan, from Rappler 2013, SunStar 2015 and Wikipedia. Stephany, Coefredo Uy’s daughter and Dexter’s sister, joins the Catbalogan Uy family. Four families were added: the Sarmiento brothers (Mel Senen, peace adviser since April 2026, and Edgar Mary), the Aquinos of Calbayog (mayor Ronaldo, assassinated 2021, and son Julius Clarence), the Calbayog Uys (Reynaldo, murdered 2011, with children Rey James and Aika) and the Zosas of Santa Margarita (Gemma and Emil). Life records added for Milagrosa Tan (30 November 2019), Ronaldo Aquino (8 March 2021) and Reynaldo Uy (2011).

### Southern Leyte

**Review status:** Evidence pass completed; the following leads remain unresolved.

- Added the former Maasin mayor to show succession rather than just today’s offices.
- 2025 results confirm the Mercados in all four provincial posts they contested: Damian (governor, 126,536 over Amalia Yap 121,334), sister Milai (vice governor, unopposed), brother Roger (1st district representative) and sister-in-law Luz (Maasin mayor, 23,443 over Maloney Samaco 22,856); Roger and Luz’s son Mikee was re-elected councilor.
- SunStar (2019) names Governor Damian as Nikko Mercado’s uncle and Mikee as his brother; Luz Mercado’s biography names both sons and Damian as her brother-in-law. Ties added accordingly.
- Coco Yap (2nd district representative) is a Mercado ally, not a recorded relative; his mother Amalia Yap was the 2025 gubernatorial challenger. Added as a separate Yap family.
- Roster extended with both representatives, the Maasin vice mayor and ten councilors, and the 18 municipal mayors from the 2026 DBM directory.

## Family evidence register

- Gerardo Espina Sr. → Rogelio Espina: **Father → son**. [Province of Biliran · About the Governor](https://biliran.gov.ph/about-the-governor/); [House of Representatives · Statement on the demise of Gerardo Espina](https://www.congress.gov.ph/index.php/media/press-releases/2744)
- Gerardo Espina Sr. → Gerardo “Gerryboy” Espina Jr.: **Father → son**. [Province of Biliran · The Congressman](https://biliran.gov.ph/the-congressman/)
### Tan — Samar

Kinship from Rappler (2013), SunStar (2015), Wikipedia and PCIJ; offices from the DBM 2026 directory and 2025 tallies. Arnold Tan is Ricardo’s brother and therefore the siblings’ uncle; a 2021 Leyte Samar Daily News report calling him a cousin is not followed. Stephany Uy-Tan is Jimboy Tan’s estranged wife and belongs to the Catbalogan Uy family; she is shown here only for that marriage link. Yolanda Tan (board member), Josie Tan (Calbayog councilor), Myrna Tan (Zumarraga) and Fe Tan-Arcales (former board member) share the surname without any published kinship and are not included.

### Ong–Ongchuan — Northern Samar

Harris and Edwin are cousins, not brothers. Emil, Jose Jr. and Hector Ong are brothers; Emil is Edwin’s father and Hector and Madeleine are Harris’s parents. Charlene is Harris’s wife. Offices are checked separately from kinship sources; the 2016 reports were published amid drug-list allegations that the family denied and that do not form part of this record.

- **Harris Christopher Ongchuan** — Northern Samar governor; former D2 representative and Laoang mayor. [DBM · Philippine Government Directory 2026](https://www.dbm.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/AboutDBM/2026-Government-Directory.pdf); [Rappler · Ongchuan cousins and Evardone father–son](https://www.rappler.com/philippines/elections/2025-races-monitor-samar/)

### Evardone — Eastern Samar

Deep audit 16 September 2026. All twelve ties were re-read against Rappler’s 2022 dynasty report and later coverage; none is contradicted. Rappler’s 2025 race monitor calls Bowad Evardone Ben’s nephew, but its 2022 report names Bowad’s father Rolando as Ben’s second cousin, so no Ben–Bowad tie is drawn. Board Member Pearl Fatima Evardone, Sulat Councilor Lulu Evardone and Hernani candidate Gerry Evardone share the surname without a stated relation.

- **Ralph Vincent “RV” Evardone** — Eastern Samar governor (since 2025); former Sulat councilor and provincial councilors’ league president. - **Ben Evardone** — Former Eastern Samar governor (2004–2010, 2019–2025) and representative (2010–2019). ### Mercado — Southern Leyte

Reviewed 15 September 2026 against Comelec 2025 results, the provincial government site and Wikipedia biographies; sibling, marriage and parent–child links are stated by Inquirer (2022), SunStar (2019), PCIJ (2025) and Luz Mercado’s biography. Top-placed 2025 Maasin councilor Isagani “Gani” Mercado shares the surname but no source states a relationship, so he is listed on the roster only. Family relationships do not imply wrongdoing or political agreement.

- **Damian “Mian” Gaviola Mercado** — Southern Leyte governor (re-elected 2025; also 2007–2013) · former lone-district representative and Maasin mayor. [Rappler · Southern Leyte 2025 results (Damian Mercado 126,536 over Amalia Yap 121,334 for governor; Milai Mercado unopposed for vice governor; Oging Mercado 68,306 for 1st district representative; Coco Yap 74,505 for 2nd district)](https://ph.rappler.com/elections/2025/local-race/southern-leyte); [Wikipedia · Damian Mercado: governor since 2016 and 2007–2013, representative 2013–2016, Maasin mayor 1998–2007; suspended August 2017–October 2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damian_Mercado); [Province of Southern Leyte · Governor Damian G. Mercado (official portrait page)](https://southernleyte.gov.ph/governor/); [PCIJ · “In Southern Leyte, the Mercado family remains firmly in power. Damian Mercado was reelected governor, sister Milai Mercado as vice governor and brother Roger Mercado as 1st District representative. Second District Rep. Coco Yap secured another term.”](https://pcij.org/2025/05/19/how-philippine-regions-voted-few-victories-versus-dynasties-but-reform-hopes-rise-for-2028-presidential-campaign/); [PSA · Southern Leyte governor and vice governor](https://rsso08.psa.gov.ph/content/psa-southern-leyte-convenes-provincial-cbms-coordinating-board-2027-cbms-preparation)

### Yap — Southern Leyte

Added 15 September 2026. Coco Yap was the Mercados’ ally as vice governor (2016–2022) and 2nd district representative; his mother Amalia Macabata Yap ran for governor against Damian Mercado in 2025 and lost narrowly. The Yaps are not recorded as relatives of the Mercados. Father Crispin Yap holds no recorded office.

- **Christopherson “Coco” Macabata Yap** — Southern Leyte 2nd district representative (re-elected 2025) · former vice governor and acting governor. [Rappler · Southern Leyte 2025 results (Damian Mercado 126,536 over Amalia Yap 121,334 for governor; Milai Mercado unopposed for vice governor; Oging Mercado 68,306 for 1st district representative; Coco Yap 74,505 for 2nd district)](https://ph.rappler.com/elections/2025/local-race/southern-leyte); [Wikipedia · Christopherson “Coco” Yap: 2nd district representative since 2022, vice governor 2016–2022, acting governor 2017–2018; son of Crispin and Amalia Yap; “In 2025, his mother, Amalia Yap ran as governor of Southern Leyte but she lost to Damian Mercado”](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopherson_Yap); [Province of Southern Leyte · Congressman, Second District: Hon. Christopherson M. Yap](https://southernleyte.gov.ph/district-ii/); [PCIJ · “In Southern Leyte, the Mercado family remains firmly in power. Damian Mercado was reelected governor, sister Milai Mercado as vice governor and brother Roger Mercado as 1st District representative. Second District Rep. Coco Yap secured another term.”](https://pcij.org/2025/05/19/how-philippine-regions-voted-few-victories-versus-dynasties-but-reform-hopes-rise-for-2028-presidential-campaign/)
- **Amalia Macabata Yap** — 2025 Southern Leyte gubernatorial candidate (independent), not elected · 121,334 votes. [Rappler · Southern Leyte 2025 results (Damian Mercado 126,536 over Amalia Yap 121,334 for governor; Milai Mercado unopposed for vice governor; Oging Mercado 68,306 for 1st district representative; Coco Yap 74,505 for 2nd district)](https://ph.rappler.com/elections/2025/local-race/southern-leyte); [Wikipedia · Christopherson “Coco” Yap: 2nd district representative since 2022, vice governor 2016–2022, acting governor 2017–2018; son of Crispin and Amalia Yap; “In 2025, his mother, Amalia Yap ran as governor of Southern Leyte but she lost to Damian Mercado”](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopherson_Yap)

## Portrait coverage

399 of 629 Visayas profiles have retrieved, attributed portraits. The other 230 remain open research gaps, not assertions that no image exists. Named Commons records and linked subject pages support the included identities. Photograph date is separate from current-office verification. All included photos are served locally, with attribution and usage information on the profile. Some historical images are lower resolution. No generated faces are used.

[Full photo credits and individual remaining gaps](/research/portrait-credits.md).

## Validation

Checks cover all 23 geographic identifiers and region assignments, 46 leadership entries, source references, unique relationship pairs, valid endpoints, current/deceased status, complete graph node placement, locally decodable photo files and share-image generation. These checks detect structural errors. They do not make the research exhaustive or guarantee that every published source is correct.
